# T30 — CROSS-PANEL TRUTH, AND THE PERMANENT LEDGER · phases P14501–P15000

Sweep #6, terminal **30 of 30** — the last one. Branch `sweep6/t30-cross-panel-and-ledger`,
worktree `~/Documents/Projects/sweep6/T30`, dev-server port **4130** (proved with
`lsof -nP -iTCP:4130 -sTCP:LISTEN` before any screenshot below was trusted).
Computed against `origin/main` **aad569aa**.

**Territory (write access):** `.claude/sweep/LEDGER/INDEX.md` · `docs/QA-500-PHASES.md` ·
`.claude/skills/terminal-test-improve/**` — and this ledger file.

**Every product fault below is a `🔗 HANDOFF`, not a fix.** This terminal owns no product
file, so where one panel disagreed with another the row names the file and the exact one-line
change instead of making it. That is §1 of the sweep rules, and it is deliberate.

**Restaurant written to:** French House only, and only ever a dish title set to its own value
and back inside one script run. **Aangan was never written to.** AV live was never read, named
or touched.

## The result of the second pass, row by row

| result | rows | what it means |
|---|---|---|
| `✅` / `✅ confirmed` / `✅ read` / `✅ captured` / `✅ fixed by this terminal` | 348 | passed. `confirmed` = watched happening on port 4130; `read` = the image was opened and looked at; plain `✅` = reasoned from the source, the LIVE schema, or the project's own written rule, and the note says which |
| `❌ → 🔗 HANDOFF` / `❌ → coverage gap` | 56 | a real problem, in a file this terminal may not write to. Every one names the file and the exact change |
| `⏭` | 96 | reserved, each with a written reason AND the command that re-derives its list — 4 of the 30 ledgers still did not exist at the end of this run, and 21 page routes and 35 screens were outside one terminal's live block |

**Why 56 rows are still `❌` after the second pass, and why that is the honest result.** This
terminal's territory is three documents. Every product fault it found lives in a file it may not
write to, so §1 of the sweep rules turns each one into a `🔗 HANDOFF` naming the file and the exact
change — it cannot be turned green from here. The 56 are: **25 handoffs** (6 distinct problems — one
browser-tab title across three files, five defects in one test guard, 73 unrenderable rows across 15
ledger files, three ledger files breaking one-id-one-check, one doc-pointer gap, one stale-count
gap), **30 coverage-gap rows** (one per orphan file, page, route or directory that no
territory was ever told to check — a fact about the sweep's own arithmetic, recorded so the next run
closes it), and **1 judgment row** answering "will the next run come back clean?" with "no". Marking
any of them `✅` would be a false green, which is exactly what made the last four sweeps look
finished when they were not.

**No row is filler.** An earlier draft of this file had 107 identical `reserved` rows; they
were replaced with one row per real file, carrying a real computed result. A `⏭` that names
the command to run is a checklist; a `⏭` that says "later" is padding.

## How to re-run this file

Re-run the whole file top to bottom — **not** only the `❌` rows. That is the instruction the
next sweep is given in `.claude/sweep/LEDGER/INDEX.md`, in this project's
`terminal-test-improve` skill, and in `docs/QA-500-PHASES.md`.

| P14924 | END TO END — a guest places an order — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | the guest's cart POSTs through app/api/guest/**; the row lands in `orders` + `order_items`; `lfh_rt_emit` publishes ops/order and ops/order_item AND table:<n>/order; the kitchen's ops handler takes the TARGETED path (`detail.tables`) and redraws that ticket alone; the tablet's ops handler does the same; the manager's ops handler calls pollTables(detail.tables) | ✅ | guest order → orders + order_items → ops + table:<n> → the manager tile, the kitchen ticket, the tablet tile. invalidateFloor(rid) at the endpoint so the writer reads its own write |
| P14925 | END TO END — a waiter takes an order on the tablet — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | the same `orders`/`order_items` rows through app/api/tablet/**, so the same breadcrumbs and the same three redraws — the kitchen cannot tell which door the order came through, and should not | ✅ | waiter order → same tables, same breadcrumb, plus withIdempotency so a retry cannot double it · s8·T10 2026-09-03: the tablet places through the shared pricing RPC and publishes through the database trigger, so the kitchen, the manager floor and the guest tracker read the same orders/order_items rows and the same breadcrumbs — no second write path exists to disagree with |
| P14926 | END TO END — the kitchen marks a dish ready — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | `order_items.status` → 'ready' publishes ops/order_item + table:<n>/order_item; the manager's floor tile recounts (items.filter(status==='ready')) and the tablet's Serve button appears for that row | ✅ | a dish marked ready → order_items.status → ops + table:<n>; every panel DERIVES the order phase from item statuses |
| P14927 | END TO END — the kitchen marks a whole ticket ready — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | every row reaches 'ready', so kitchen `orderPhase()` returns 'ready' and the ticket moves to the ready column — a DERIVED phase, not a fourth stored status | ✅ | a whole ticket ready → the same rows in one write; the manager clamps `ready`→`preparing` before writing an ORDER status |
| P14928 | END TO END — the manager settles the bill — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | `sessions.status` → 'closed' plus `orders.payment_status` → 'paid'; mig 299's rt_emit_sessions watches status, bill_no, invoice_no, closed_at and deleted_at, so ops/session publishes; the floor tile frees, the tablet's tile frees, and the kitchen (which does not render money) is untouched | ✅ | settle → sessions.status + orders.payment_status, both watch-listed → ops + table:<n> |
| P14929 | END TO END — the manager settles a bill in PARTS — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | `session_payments` gets a row; mig 267 gave it its own kind (ops/payment) scoped to that table, so only that tile refetches | ✅ | settle in parts → session_payments → ops + table:<n> (mig 267), so ONE tile refetches |
| P14930 | END TO END — the owner reads it in a report — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | `lfh_owner_sales_report` sums the DATABASE's stored `net_amount`, the same figure `netOf()` returns to the admin ledger | ✅ | the owner reads it in a report → no breadcrumb by design; lib/ownerCache.ts is fingerprint-gated and Refresh forces live |
| P14931 | END TO END — the admin sees it in the bill ledger — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | app/api/admin/bills builds each row through lib/billLedger's `deriveBillState()` and `netOf()`, and reports live and deleted counts separately | ✅ | the admin bill ledger → components/admin/shared.tsx, the only subscriber on all three topics |
| P14932 | END TO END — a bill is CANCELLED — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | `deriveBillState` returns 'cancelled' for a closed session with nothing collected; the compliance rule keeps it in the Z-report at ₹0 and names it beside the money collected | ✅ | a cancel → the sale stays at ₹0 with its reason, person and time, and an audit row names all four |
| P14933 | END TO END — a bill is soft-DELETED (binned) — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | the admin ledger shows it as state 'deleted' with its own count and total; the owner's revenue STILL includes it; the pay-later book STOPS asking for it | ✅ | a soft delete → sessions.deleted_at IS watch-listed, so the tile updates; the money still counts as collected and drops out of owed |
| P14934 | END TO END — the admin switches a guest feature OFF — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | app/api/admin/restaurants/access-tree writes `settings` and `restaurants`; `settings` publishes menu/settings, `restaurants` publishes menu/access (mig 299 §B), and the route ALSO calls `revalidateTag(menuTag(rid), {expire: 0})` at line 379 to drop the server-side menu bundle | ✅ | the admin switches a feature off → settings → menu/settings → refreshFeatures(rid) in the browser, bounded by the 8s server TTL |
| P14935 | END TO END — a guest's open phone hears that switch — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | components/AppShell.tsx subscribes with `filter: topic_rid=eq.menu:<rid>` and calls refresh() on ANY menu-topic breadcrumb; components/MenuView.tsx's useRealtime({menu}) refreshes the menu AND the features | ✅ | a guest's open phone hears it → RealtimeProvider on table:<n>:<rid> and AppShell on menu:<rid> — WATCHED landing in ~1s this run (a dish edit appeared on an already-open guest menu in ~1000ms) · re-run 2026-09-02 (sweep 8, T5) |
| P14936 | END TO END — the admin takes a power off ONE manager — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | mig 320's rt_emit on `staff_users.permissions`, gated by IS DISTINCT FROM, publishes so that person's open panel stops offering the control | ✅ | a power taken off one manager → staff_users.permissions → menu/access (mig 320) → refreshWhoami() on the open panel |
| P14937 | END TO END — the owner edits a dish price — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | app/api/editor busts menuTag(rid) at line 530 AND the DB publishes menu/menu_item; the guest's cached bundle is dropped server-side, the guest refetches, the tablet flags `_menuStale` and does a FULL load so a just-sold-out dish becomes untappable mid-order | ✅ | a dish price edited → menu_items → menu/menu_item, and revalidateTag(menuTag(rid)) purges the guest bundle in the same endpoint. WATCHED this run: all three staff panels received the breadcrumb within ~700ms at ~140ms latency and each refetched |
| P14938 | END TO END — the owner renames a table — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | mig 299 added `table_number` to the sessions watch-list precisely so a party moved by anything OTHER than lfh_staff_shift_table still publishes; without it the party showed on two tables at once until the 60s backstop | ✅ | a table renamed → settings.table_names → menu/settings, and the label reaches the printed KOT as well as the screen |
| P14939 | END TO END — a restaurant's web address is reused by a new restaurant — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | mig 350 keeps the old address finding the restaurant; the create route records in the permanent audit that the printed QR codes now open THIS menu | ✅ | a reused web address → lib/tenant.ts movedSlug + mig 350: a vacant slug redirects 307, a reused one warns |
| P14940 | END TO END — a staff action lands in the activity log — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | `staff_actions` publishes audit/action — its OWN topic since mig 267, so only components/admin/shared.tsx wakes | ✅ | a staff action → the audit topic ONLY (mig 267), so no floor tile pays for it. WATCHED: "received audit/action in 102ms" |
| P14941 | END TO END — a complaint is raised — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | mig 351 gives a complaint its printer, so it reaches paper on the right device | ✅ | a complaint → printer_events/issues → ops → the manager strip and the admin console on the same breadcrumb |
| P14942 | END TO END — a device goes offline mid-order — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | the write goes to the panel outbox (X-LFH-Action-Id + withIdempotency), and on reconnect it drains; `LFH_RT.catchUp()` closes the gap in what the screen missed | ✅ | offline mid-order → the write is queued through api()/the guest outbox with an action id and replays idempotently |
| P14943 | END TO END — a KOT is reprinted — every panel it touches tells the SAME story | no band on the bill, no audit row, no question — the reprint is not an event; the KOT banner and the reopen audit both stay | ✅ | a KOT reprint → print_jobs publishes for reprints ONLY (mig 335), and the paper carries the DUPLICATE banner |

# SWEEP #7 RE-RUN AND NEW BLOCK — terminal 30 of 40 · cross-panel truth

**Re-run: 2026-08-28, against `origin/main` c005b3d3.** Branch `sweep7/t30-cross-panel-truth`,
worktree `~/Documents/Projects/wt-s7-t30`, dev server on port **4230** (proved with
`lsof -nP -iTCP:4230 -sTCP:LISTEN` and `/api/health` → 200 before any screenshot was trusted).

**Territory this time:** cross-panel truth — when something changes in one panel, does every other
panel that must show it actually get it (breadcrumb column, targeted refetch, `invalidateFloor`,
`boardSig` repaint), and do the panels that must NOT see it stay blind?

## The re-run of P14501–P15000 — every row, top to bottom

| | |
|---|---|
| Rows re-run | **500 of 500** |
| **REGRESSIONS** | **0** |
| Rows that were ❌ and are now green | **57** — 25 handoffs and 30 coverage gaps, plus two arithmetic rows |
| Rows still ❌ | **1** — `P14543`, unchanged since sweep #6, and it is a duplicated money RULE, not a wrong number |
| Rows reserved (`⏭`) | 21, each naming the command that re-derives its list |
| Expectations that legitimately MOVED (id kept, text sharpened) | `P14501` · `P14509` · `P14794` · `P14799` · `P14983` |

**What closed, and why it is worth writing down.** Sweep #6's T30 could fix nothing — its territory
was three documents — so every product fault it found became a `🔗 HANDOFF`. All six are now done
on `main`: the three twin browser-tab titles (T29, with `verify-twin-route-parity.mjs` to keep
them), the five `verify:realtime` defects (the guard was RUN this run: ALL PASS, 5 breadcrumbs
delivered), and `verify:pointers` (green). And every one of the 30 coverage gaps is filled —
sweep #7 gives the eight unowned shared components to T31, the five orphan page routes and two
orphan API routes to T32, the migration tail to T33 and the three unowned directories to T34.

**Three of my own detectors were wrong before the product was**, and each is recorded in its row so
nobody re-files it: the ledger-render check (25 hits, all legitimate recap tables — a phase row is
identified by its SHAPE), the "this cache has an invalidator" check (it wanted `export function`
and missed `export async function`), and the "a manager can hide a sale" grep (five hits, every one
a comment, and `verify:one-bill-delete` asserts the rule directly).

## The new block — P29601–P30100

| block | ids | rows | what it is |
|-------|-----|------|------------|
| **A** · the breadcrumb ledger, re-derived from the LIVE database | P29601–P29700 | 100 | every trigger, every kind, every topic, every subscriber, the per-restaurant scoping, and the two kinds that are new since sweep #6 |
| **B** · every write endpoint — does it publish, and does it bust? | P29701–P29795 | 95 | the columns the app writes vs the columns each trigger fires on · the floor drop · idempotency · the admin's and the owner's writes reaching a restaurant's screens |
| **C** · the panels that must stay BLIND | P29796–P29868 | 73 | the private settings column · what a guest hears · a waiter's own section · what the owner never sees · each restaurant only its own numbers |
| **D** · caches in front of breadcrumbs | P29869–P29941 | 73 | all 20 module-level caches re-derived · every settings/menu writer · the chain from a write to a repaint, one row per surface · the 16 known traps |
| **E** · watched happening, headless on 4230 | P29942–P29976 | 35 | a dish edit landing on an already-open guest menu in ~1s, and all three staff panels receiving the same breadcrumb at ~140ms with their own metrics read from inside the panel |
| **F** · screenshots captured and actually looked at | P29977–P30026 | 50 | 8 images opened and read with a picky eye, 29 more captured as a baseline |
| **G** · my own judgment, and the end-to-end stories | P30027–P30100 | 74 | 24 end-to-end traces, 26 judgments, and reserved rows that each name their command |

**Result of the new block:** 100 `✅ confirmed` · 319 `✅` · 1 `❌` · 1 `✅ NOT a finding` · 30 `⏭` · 20 `✅ read` · 29 `✅ captured`.

**Follow-up rows `P30101`–`P30105`** were added on 2026-08-29 when the owner asked for three of the
report's items to be acted on and one re-checked. They are OUTSIDE the P29601–P30100 block on
purpose: they are new checks, not re-runs, and each names the command that produced its answer.

**The one finding:** `P29711` — `sessions.bill_printed_at` is the only column the app writes on a
watch-listed table with no reason recorded for being outside that table's breadcrumb watch-list. Its
consequence is a button label, not money and not paper: a bill printed at the till leaves a second
device's button reading "🖨 Print" instead of "🖨 Reprint" until its 60-second backstop.

| P30027 | END TO END — a guest places an order: every panel that must show it does, and every panel that must not, does not | trace it by reading, hop by hop: the table written → the live trigger → the kind and topic → the subscribers → the handler → the payload field the screen renders | ✅ | orders + order_items → ops + table:<n> → the manager tile, the kitchen ticket and the tablet tile; invalidateFloor(rid) at the endpoint so the writer reads its own write |
| P30028 | END TO END — a waiter takes an order on the tablet: every panel that must show it does, and every panel that must not, does not | trace it by reading, hop by hop: the table written → the live trigger → the kind and topic → the subscribers → the handler → the payload field the screen renders | ✅ | the same tables, the same breadcrumb, and withIdempotency so a retry on a flaky tablet cannot double it |
| P30029 | END TO END — the kitchen marks a dish ready: every panel that must show it does, and every panel that must not, does not | trace it by reading, hop by hop: the table written → the live trigger → the kind and topic → the subscribers → the handler → the payload field the screen renders | ✅ | order_items.status → ops + table:<n>; every panel DERIVES the order phase from its item statuses rather than storing a fourth status |
| P30030 | END TO END — the kitchen marks a whole ticket ready: every panel that must show it does, and every panel that must not, does not | trace it by reading, hop by hop: the table written → the live trigger → the kind and topic → the subscribers → the handler → the payload field the screen renders | ✅ | the same rows in one write; the manager clamps `ready` to `preparing` before writing an ORDER status so the live constraint holds |
| P30031 | END TO END — the manager settles the bill: every panel that must show it does, and every panel that must not, does not | trace it by reading, hop by hop: the table written → the live trigger → the kind and topic → the subscribers → the handler → the payload field the screen renders | ✅ | sessions.status + orders.payment_status → ops + table:<n>; the tile outline goes green on every device |
| P30032 | END TO END — the manager settles a bill in PARTS: every panel that must show it does, and every panel that must not, does not | trace it by reading, hop by hop: the table written → the live trigger → the kind and topic → the subscribers → the handler → the payload field the screen renders | ✅ | session_payments → ops + table:<n> (mig 267), so ONE tile refetches instead of the whole floor |
| P30033 | END TO END — the owner reads it in a report: every panel that must show it does, and every panel that must not, does not | trace it by reading, hop by hop: the table written → the live trigger → the kind and topic → the subscribers → the handler → the payload field the screen renders | ✅ | no breadcrumb by design — lib/ownerCache.ts is a fingerprint-gated snapshot and Refresh forces live |

A HARD delete is refused by lfh_block_issued_delete — every order carries a KOT number from the moment it exists — and that refusal is the compliance guardrail working, so the sanctioned soft delete was used instead, with deleted_by and a delete_reason naming why. The 6-hour cutoff left 24 rows that a currently-running terminal may still be using. No money moved: all 192 were unpaid, and lfh_owner_sales_report sums only payment_status='paid'. |
| P30105 | the seeded demo history was NOT touched by that cleanup | break the orders down by month before deleting anything: select date_trunc('month',created_at), count(*), count(*) filter (where archived) | ✅ confirmed | CHECKED FIRST, AND IT CHANGED THE PLAN: June and July hold 8,166 session-less orders and every one is ARCHIVED — they are the practice restaurant's seeded back-catalogue, the thing the owner's reports read. The naive predicate "session_id IS NULL" would have taken all of them. `NOT archived` is what makes the cleanup safe, and it is why the candidate set was 192 and not 8,605. |

# ROUND 2 — the owner asked for a fresh 500 inside the same boundaries · P43501–P44000

**Written and executed 2026-08-30**, against `origin/main` a0f0d69a (this terminal's own first
round, merged and live on backup earlier the same day). Branch `sweep7/t30-round2-cross-panel`,
dev server on port **4230** (proved with `lsof` before anything on it was trusted).

**Same territory, deliberately different ground.** Round 1 mapped the plumbing — every trigger,
kind, topic, subscriber and cache. Round 2 asks the question round 1 stopped one step short of:
**what does the targeted path NOT refresh?** That is where the fault was.

| block | ids | rows | what it is |
|-------|-----|------|------------|
| **A** · the three redraw fingerprints | P43501–P43542 | 42 | kitchen, tablet AND the manager's Bills fingerprint — which no guard had ever watched — every collection each one covers, and the guard's own health |
| **B** · the targeted refetch, and what it leaves behind | P43543–P43583 | 41 | one row per panel for what its `?table=N` path fetches, refreshes, and does NOT refresh — and whether each omission is deliberate |
| **C** · the guest's own cross-panel truth | P43584–P43640 | 57 | one socket, one debounced signal, six listeners · 22 guest surfaces × the change that must reach them · 10 things a guest must never receive · the three doors |
| **D+E** · the admin looking through a panel, and offline → online | P43641–P43710 | 70 | the x-ray ribbon in all three panels, the per-tab restaurant pin · the outbox, the action id, the replay, and what the other panels see meanwhile |
| **F** · the event × surface matrix | P43711–P43890 | 180 | 27 real events × 6 surfaces, each cell derived from the trigger's topic, the surface's subscriptions and whether it renders the thing — plus 18 money-agreement rows |
| **live** · watched happening on 4230 | P43891–P43896 | 6 | one probe order on its own table, watched on three panels at once |
| **H** · the safety nets, judgment, and the failure map | P43897–P44000 | 104 | the backstop/catch-up/backoff per panel · the owner console's deliberate silence · the status vocabulary · 20 judgments · 24 reserved rows that each name their command · a 12-row "if this broke, look here first" map |

**Result:** 432 `✅` · 30 `⏭` · 20 `✅ deliberately blind` · 14 `✅ confirmed` · 3 `✅ NOT a finding` · 1 `✅ fixed by this terminal`.

## What this round found

**ONE fault, and it is fixed on this branch.** `P43559` — the manager's **Bills → Live list kept a
bill's old status for up to a minute after the floor tile beside it had already moved.** Every
order change names its table, so realtime takes the targeted path, and that path refreshes the
tiles, the aggregates and any open table detail — all of which are the FLOOR. The Bills list is a
different screen built from `state.data.orders`, whose only writers are whole-board reads and the
per-table slice merge. Nothing on the targeted path touched it.

**It took three measurements to state honestly.** The first two counted bill cards, and ten sweep
terminals write to this shared restaurant — the count moved under the test in both directions and
gave first a false green and then a false red. The third used a probe order with a title no other
terminal can produce: it read "NEW ORDER" for 30 seconds after the database said `preparing`, then
flipped the moment the 60-second backstop landed. With the fix, ~3–4 seconds, watched on three
panels at once.

**The fix keeps the egress shape.** It widens the existing `toRefresh` list and reuses
`loadTableSlice()` — three `?table=N` reads for the tables that actually changed — never
`pollOrders()`. The targeted path exists because whole-board reads were ~96% of this product's
egress; "fix the list by reloading the board" would hand that straight back. It is gated on the tab
actually being open on the live view, so a manager on the Tables tab pays nothing.

**And the guard that should have caught it has been widened twice.** `verify:board-sig` knew about
two panels of three, and its "the fingerprint is built from stableRow" check passed on a single
mention — so a fingerprint narrowed from seven collections to one would have stayed green. It now
covers the manager, asserts each fingerprint still reaches every collection it covered, and refuses
a whole-board read on the targeted path. Every new check was proved to fail on the fault it
describes before being kept.

**Printing was not touched** (owner, 2026-08-30: keep the printing logic exactly as it stands).

| id | check | how to verify | result | note |

| P43921 | JUDGMENT — the one fault this round found is the shape worth hunting for | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | a screen whose data has ONLY whole-board writers, sitting behind a targeted refetch. The floor got all the attention because the floor is what a tile is; the list beside it was built from a different array and nobody joined the two facts up. |
| P43922 | JUDGMENT — and it was invisible to every existing guard | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | verify:board-sig knew about two panels of three. verify:floor-share guards the SERVER's shared read. Nothing asserted "the targeted path feeds every screen that is open". |
| P43923 | JUDGMENT — the fix had to keep the egress shape, and that constraint改 improved it | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | the lazy fix is pollOrders() on every targeted event, which hands back the whole-board read that was ~96% of this product's egress. Reusing loadTableSlice keeps it to three scoped reads for the tables that actually changed, and only while that tab is open. |
| P43924 | JUDGMENT — measuring on a shared database nearly cost me two false findings | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | a count of cards moved under the test in BOTH directions while ten terminals wrote to the same restaurant. The first run said "no gap" and the second said "gap" — both were noise. A probe row only this run can create is the only honest instrument here. |
| P43925 | JUDGMENT — and the control is what made the third measurement trustworthy | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | "did the 60s backstop fix it?" is the question that separates a real gap from a card that never renders that field at all. Without it I would have filed the second reading. |
| P43926 | JUDGMENT — a guard that passes on one mention of a helper is barely a guard | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | "boardSig is built from stableRow" was satisfied by a single call. A fingerprint narrowed from seven collections to one would have stayed green — the same regression the file exists to stop, one level up. |
| P43927 | JUDGMENT — three fingerprints ship and only two were watched | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | the manager panel is the busiest of the three and had no coverage at all. Counting the things that exist is worth more than checking the ones you already know about. |
| P43928 | JUDGMENT — the guest side is the cleanest part of this territory | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | one socket, one debounced signal, six listeners that all unsubscribe. Nothing in it needed a fix this round. |
| P43929 | JUDGMENT — the deliberate blindness is as carefully built as the delivery | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | the heartbeat, the cart, the bill counter, the ordinary print job and the oplog are each excluded on purpose, each with a migration header saying why. That is what keeps this product quiet. |
| P43930 | JUDGMENT — the targeted breadcrumb is the single best decision in this area | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | without the `table:<n>` second row, every guest order would cost every open device a whole-floor read. |
| P43931 | JUDGMENT — and it is also where the faults live | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | everything that goes wrong here goes wrong because the targeted path is narrower than the screen. That is the trade the design makes, so it is the trade to keep auditing. |
| P43932 | JUDGMENT — the 60-second backstop is why none of this is ever a disaster | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | every gap in this territory degrades to "a minute late", never to "wrong forever". It is the reason this round's fault was worth fixing but never worth panicking about. |
| P43933 | JUDGMENT — the shared dev database is now the biggest obstacle to honest testing here | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | not the code. Ten terminals writing to one restaurant makes every aggregate assertion unusable. |
| P43934 | JUDGMENT — cleaning up rows by id is not bureaucracy, it is what makes the next measurement possible | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | the 192 abandoned rows removed this session were the same rows that made a kitchen board unreadable for anyone trying to eyeball it. |
| P43935 | JUDGMENT — the compliance trigger refusing my delete was the system working | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | every order carries a KOT number from birth, so nothing can be hard-deleted. The right answer was the soft delete the admin console itself uses, not the purge override. |
| P43936 | JUDGMENT — printing was left exactly as it stands, on instruction, and nothing here needed it | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | the one printing-adjacent item found earlier is recorded and untouched. |
| P43937 | JUDGMENT — the money story needed no fix this round | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | one generated column, one netOf(), and now a scan that catches both shapes of a hand-worked net. Across every live order on the dev database the two definitions disagree zero times. |
| P43938 | JUDGMENT — what I would watch next in this territory | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | the tablet's two-tier design — the grid draws from the slim summary and the detail from a full slice. That is the same "two sources, one screen" shape that produced this round's fault. |
| P43939 | JUDGMENT — and the second thing | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | the owner console, which deliberately has no breadcrumb at all and relies on a fingerprint-gated snapshot. Nothing tested what happens when that fingerprint does not move but the data did. |
| P43940 | JUDGMENT — a re-run is still worth more than a new idea | my own reading after re-running this territory and measuring it live | ✅ | this round's fault came from following one existing question — "what does the targeted path NOT refresh?" — one step further than last round did. |
